\subsubsection{Schedule}

As mentioned in earlier sections the order of execution of messages plays an important role in the message passing scheme. In this section the agent largely relies on Bethe messages. In this case messages regularly depend on other messages which makes a correct schedule indispensable (later sections present other types of messages that allow for a lesser strict schedule). The schedule for the agent's temporal generative model in \cref{fig:18} is given in \cref{alg:2}.
\begin{algorithm}[htbp]
    \caption{Message Passing Algorithm of the BFE Agent}
    \label{alg:2}
    \begin{algorithmic}[1] % Die [1] sorgt für automatische Zeilennummern!
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}o_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}u_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}o_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}u_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}o_2\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}o_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}o_1\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s_2\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_1\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s_1\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}u_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_0\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}u_1\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}s_2\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s_0\bm{)}}$, $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_0\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}o_2\bm{)}}$
        \State \textbf{evaluate} $q(s_0)$, $q(s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_0)$, $q(s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_0)$, $q(s_1)$, $q(s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_1)$, $q(s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_1)$, $q(s_2)$, $q(u_0)$, $q(u_1)$, $q(o_0)$, $q(o_1)$,
        \Statex \phantom{\textbf{evaluate} }$q(o_2)$
    \end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}


\begin{appendices}

    \chapter{Equality Node Messages}
    \label[appendix]{app:1}

    In this section I present the messages sent by equality nodes. As mentioned before edges in the factor graph are allowed to have a maximum number of connected nodes of two. This is a technical problem because there are variables (edges) that occur in more than two factor function (nodes). As a remedy factor graphs support equality nodes that artificially duplicate the variables and enforce an equality relation between the duplicated variables. This relation is implemented in the factor function of these nodes as a product of Kronecker (or Dirac) delta functions. As an example consider the the factor graph in \cref{fig:13} where we are interested in \(\textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}}\), \(\textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}}\), and \(\textcolor{orange}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}}\).
    \begin{figure}[htbp]
        \centering

        \begin{tikzpicture}[
                factor/.style={draw=black, thick, rectangle, minimum size=0.8cm,
                        font=\normalsize},
                eqfactor/.style={draw=black, thick, rectangle, minimum size=0.5cm, font=\normalsize},
                edge/.style={thick},
                varlabel/.style={font=\normalsize}
            ]

            % Knoten
            \node[factor, label={[varlabel]below:$q_a(x)$}] (a) {$f_a(x)$};
            \node[eqfactor, label={[varlabel]above:$q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})$}, right=4cm of a] (b) {$=$};
            \node[factor, label={[varlabel]below:$q_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})$}, below=3cm of b] (d) {$f_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})$};
            \node[factor, label={[varlabel]below:$q_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})$}, right=4cm of b] (c) {$f_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})$};

            % Kanten
            \draw[edge] (a.east) -- (b.west) node[midway, above, varlabel] {$x$} node[midway, below, varlabel] {$q_i(x)$} node[pos=0.15, below, varlabel] {$\textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}}$} node[pos=0.85, below, varlabel] {$\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}}$};
            \draw[edge] (b.south) -- (d.north) node[midway, left, varlabel] {$x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}$} node[midway, right, varlabel] {$q_k(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})$} node[pos=0.82, right=0.05, varlabel] {$\textcolor{orange}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}}$} node[pos=0.18, right=0.05, varlabel] {$\textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}}$};
            \draw[edge] (b.east) -- (c.west) node[midway, above, varlabel] {$x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}$} node[midway, below, varlabel] {$q_j(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})$} node[pos=0.85, below, varlabel] {$\textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}}$} node[pos=0.15, below, varlabel] {$\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}}$};

        \end{tikzpicture}

        \caption{Example FFG with messages sent by the equality node (\textcolor{orange}{orange}).}
        \label{fig:13}
    \end{figure}
    The factor function of the equality node is given by
    \begin{equation}
        f_= = \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}}.
    \end{equation}
    This function is a discrete probability distribution that has probability 1 only if the values of all variables are equal. In other cases it is 0.
    Building on this the node local free energy is
    \begin{equation}
        F_{=} = \sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \log \frac{q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})}{\delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}}}.
    \end{equation}
    To determine the messages next we need the free energy functional of the factor graph in \cref{fig:13} with
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{split}
            F = & \sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \log \frac{q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})}{\delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}}} + \sum_x q_a(x) \log \frac{q_a(x)}{f_a(x)} + \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} q_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) \log \frac{q_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})}{f_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})} \\
                & + \sum_z q_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \log \frac{q_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})}{f_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})} + \sum_x q_i(x) \log \frac{1}{q_i(x)} + \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} q_j(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) \log \frac{1}{q_j(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})}                                                                                                                                                                                     \\
                & + \sum_z q_k(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \log \frac{1}{q_k(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})}.
        \end{split}
    \end{equation}
    Adding marginalization and normalization constraints to \(F\) leads to the Lagrangian
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{split}
            L = & \; F + \sum_x \lambda_{i=}(x) (q_i(x) - \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})) + \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \lambda_{j=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) (q_j(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) - \sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})) \\
                & + \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \lambda_{k=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) (q_k(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) - \sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})) + \sum_x \lambda_{ia}(x) (q_i(x) - q_a(x))                                                                                                                 \\
                & + \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \lambda_{jb}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) (q_j(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) - q_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})) + \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \lambda_{kc}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) (q_k(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) - q_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}))                                                                                \\
                & + \psi_= (\sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) - 1) + \psi_a (\sum_x q_a(x) - 1) + \psi_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} (\sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} q_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) - 1)                                                                                              \\
                & + \psi_c (\sum_z q_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) - 1) + \psi_i (\sum_x q_i(x) - 1) + \psi_j (\sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} q_j(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) - 1) + \psi_k (\sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_k(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) - 1)
        \end{split}
    \end{equation}
    Solving the belief distributions in \(L\) for the stationary points then yields
    \begin{alignat}{3}
         & \frac{\delta L}{\delta q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})} &  & = \log q_=(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) + 1 - \log (\delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}}) - \lambda_{i=}(x) - \lambda_{j=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) - \lambda_{k=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) + \psi_=       & \; \overset{!}{=} 0 \\
         &                                                                                   &  & \Rightarrow q_=^*(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \propto \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \exp(\lambda_{i=}(x)) \exp(\lambda_{j=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})) \exp(\lambda_{k=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})).
    \end{alignat}
    Similarly
    \begin{alignat}{3}
         & \Rightarrow q_a^*(x) \propto f_a(x) \exp(\lambda_{ia}(x))                                                                                         \\
         & \Rightarrow q_b^*(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) \propto f_b(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) \exp(\lambda_{jb}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}))                    \\
         & \Rightarrow q_c^*(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \propto f_c(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \exp(\lambda_{kc}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}))                 \\
         & \Rightarrow q_i^*(x) \propto \exp(\lambda_{ia}(x)) \exp(\lambda_{i=}(x))                                                                          \\
         & \Rightarrow q_j^*(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}) \propto \exp(\lambda_{j=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})) \exp(\lambda_{jb}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}))     \\
         & \Rightarrow q_k^*(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \propto \exp(\lambda_{k=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})) \exp(\lambda_{kc}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})).
    \end{alignat}
    Next, we use the marginalization constraint \(\sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=^*(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \overset{!}{=} q_i^*(x)\) to solve for the message sent by the equality node to node \(a\) (via edge \(i\)) with
    \begin{alignat}{2}
         & \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=^*(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        &  & \overset{!}{=} q_i^*(x)                             \\
         & \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \exp(\lambda_{i=}(x)) \exp(\lambda_{j=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})) \exp(\lambda_{k=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}))                                                                                                                          &  & \propto \exp(\lambda_{ia}(x)) \exp(\lambda_{i=}(x)) \\
         & \Rightarrow \exp(\lambda_{ia}(x))              \propto \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \exp(\lambda_{j=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})) \exp(\lambda_{k=}(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}))                                                                                                                                                  \\
         & \Rightarrow \textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}} \propto \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}} \label{eq:21}                                                          \\
         & \left(\Rightarrow \textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}} \propto \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}x\bm{)}}\right). \label{eq:22}
    \end{alignat}
    Analogously using the constraints \(\sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} q_=^*(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \overset{!}{=} q_j^*(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I})\) and \(\sum_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} q_=^*(x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}) \overset{!}{=} q_k^*(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II})\) we obtain
    \begin{alignat}{2}
         & \Rightarrow \textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}} \propto \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}}   \\
         & \Rightarrow \textcolor{orange}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}} \propto \sum_{x^{\scriptscriptstyle I},x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{x,x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}}.
    \end{alignat}
    This reveals that outgoing messages from equality nodes (e.g., \(\textcolor{orange}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}}\) in \cref{eq:21}) are the product of incoming messages (\(\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\bm{)}}\) in \cref{eq:21}) (except from the neighoring node that is the addresse of the message to be sent) where variables of incoming messages get substituted with the variable of the outgoing message as a result of the summation and the two Kronecker deltas. While in principle this substitution leads to \cref{eq:22} I refrain from that further step because the resulting messages (\(\textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}x\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}x\bm{)}}\)) might be misunderstood for the messages that are actually sent on the edge of variable \(x\). Instead they really are the incoming messages from the edges of variables \(x^{\scriptscriptstyle I}\) and \(x^{\scriptscriptstyle II}\) respectively where the variables got substituted with \(x\).
    Relating back to the previous section the messages sent by equality nodes are Bethe messages with the constraint of having a product of Kronecker deltas as the factor function.

    Following this the messages sent by equality nodes in the factor graph of the temporal generative model (\cref{fig:14}) can now be clearly stated with

    \noindent
    \begin{minipage}[t]{0.5\textwidth}
        \setlength{\abovedisplayskip}{0pt}
        \setlength{\belowdisplayskip}{0pt}
        \begin{alignat}{2}
             & \textcolor{blue}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s_0\bm{)}}                         &  & = \sum_{s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle I},s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{s_0,s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{s_0,s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_0\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_0\bm{)}} \\
             & \textcolor{blue}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_0\bm{)}} &  & = \sum_{s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle I},s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{s_0,s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{s_0,s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s_0\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_0\bm{)}}                       \\
             & \textcolor{blue}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_0\bm{)}}   &  & = \sum_{s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle I},s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{s_0,s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{s_0,s_0^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s_0\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_0\bm{)}}
        \end{alignat}
    \end{minipage}\hfill
    \begin{minipage}[t]{0.5\textwidth}
        \setlength{\abovedisplayskip}{0pt}
        \setlength{\belowdisplayskip}{0pt}
        \begin{alignat}{2}
             & \textcolor{blue}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s_1\bm{)}}                         &  & = \sum_{s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle I},s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{s_1,s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{s_1,s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_1\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_1\bm{)}} \\
             & \textcolor{blue}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_1\bm{)}} &  & = \sum_{s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle I},s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{s_1,s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{s_1,s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s_1\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{{\uparrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_1\bm{)}}                       \\
             & \textcolor{blue}{\bm{{\downarrow}{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle II}_1\bm{)}}   &  & = \sum_{s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle I},s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \delta_{s_1,s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle I}} \delta_{s_1,s_1^{\scriptscriptstyle II}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overrightarrow{\mu}(}s_1\bm{)}} \textcolor{black}{\bm{\overleftarrow{\mu}(}s^{\scriptscriptstyle I}_1\bm{)}}
        \end{alignat}
    \end{minipage}
    \vspace{1em}
